2. RECOGNITION 
  A.  2013 – 2014 Senior Scholar Athletes  
It is a pleasure this evening to welcome and acknowledge our senior 
scholar athletes from winter and spring sports.  To achieve this 
distinction, a student must maintain a 3.5 cumulative grade point 
average while participating in an athletic program.   
As your name is called, please come forward.

2.       RECOGNITION 
C. Principal Appreciation Month 
Throughout the State of Michigan, February has traditionally been celebrated as 
Principal Appreciation Month, in recognition of the outstanding work of our 
building-level educational leaders. In Troy, we are fortunate to have an 
exemplary group of principals dedicated to the success of every student in the 
District. This evening, we will pass a resolution to commend our elementary and 
secondary principals for their ex ceptional commitment to excellence in the Troy 
School District.  
WHEREAS, energetic and inspiring school leadership is essential if teachers, 
schools and districts are to ensure that all Troy students are college - and 
career-ready; and, 
WHEREAS, K-12 school principals play a vital role in the success of students 
and act as the liaison between the school and the community it serves, ensuring 
that parents and the community are aware of student and school achievements; 
and, 
WHEREAS, Troy School District p rincipals are dedicated,  quality educational 
professionals committed to providing sound leadership and guidance to their 
staff and students; and,  
WHEREAS, during this month, we join with educators, parents and students 
throughout Michigan to raise aware ness of the importance of educational 
leadership, and to recognize and thank all principals working in Troy for their 
exemplary service; 
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED , that the Troy Board of Education 
officially recognizes February as Principal Appreciation Month in Troy and thanks 
our dedicated building leaders for all they do for our students and staff.    
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Read and adopt resolution. 
  Moved by:     
  Supported by:     
Yes:      
No:

2.       RECOGNITION 
D. Enviro-Clean Donation to Troy Foundation 
This evening we would like to welcome two representatives from Enviro -Clean 
Maintenance Services - John Thompson, Director of Educational Services  and 
Craig Wyse, Assistant Director of Educational Services.  They  have joined us 
tonight to show their commitment to Troy Schools and the educational process 
by donating a check, in the amount of $5,000, to the Troy Foundation for 
Educational Excellence.  Mr. Al Robinson, president of TFEE, is here to represent 
the Foundation and accept this donation.    
_____________________________________________________________ 
RECOMMENDATION:  Invite John Thompson and Craig Wyse to present the check to Al 
Robinson.

School Challenge, which is a national event sponsored by 
Sodexo.   The  event  was  created  to  further  the  nutrition 
education  of  high  school  students  and  to  build  ongoing 
excitement  around  the  Sodexo  food  service  programs.  
High  school  nutrition  programs  managed  by  Sodexo 
around  the  country  competed  in  the  challenge,  earning 
points for served meals and healthy ala carte snacks and 
beverages.  Schools  serving  a  higher  number  of  meals, 
healthy  snacks  and  beverages  during  the  challenge 
earned  more  points.   The  challenge  took  place  during 
September  and  October  of  2013.  The  winner  of  this 
challenge  was  the  International  Academy‐East.   Mr.  Hal 
Davis presented a check for $500 to Needa Toofanny, IA‐
East student representative to the Board.

       ATHENS HIGH SCHOOL           
  President  Philippart  called  the  meeting  to  order  at  7:25 
p.m. 
In  addition  to  Ms.  Philippart,  present  were  Board 
members Edmunds, Fleming, Hauff, Miletti, Schmidt and 
Underwood.   Also  present  were  Superintendent  Fowler;

RECOGNITION  
the  Troy  School  District  recognized  a  very  important 
group  of  individuals  who  are  tireless,  professional,  and 
passionate  about  improving  the  opportunities  of  our 
students  –  a  group  that  has  given  so  much  to  our 
students  and  staff:  The  Board  of  Education.   Board 
members  were  recognized  with  a  gift  to  represent  the 
community they give so much to.   They were presented 
with a small token of appreciation to allow them to enjoy 
what  is  unique  about  Troy ‐  a  meal  out  at  one of  Troy’s 
newest restaurants and a couple keepsakes to show city 
pride.  The Board was thanked for all they do.

A. CONSIDERATION:  BEST PRACTICES INCENTIVE 
  Attached is a resolution for Board consideration 
regarding the 2013-2014 Best Practices Incentive , 
Section 22f  with the Michigan Department of 
Education. 
________________________________________________________ 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the resolution below. 
 Moved by:       __________________________ 
 Supported by:  __________________________ 
WHEREAS, Section 22f of the State School Aid Act provides 
$52 per pupil one -time grants to districts that satisfy at least 7 of 8 
best practices criteria not later than June 1, 201 4 (MCL 388.1622f); 
and; 
WHEREAS, the Board of Education of Troy School District 
desires to receive the $52 per pupil incentive payment; and; 
WHEREAS, the Troy School District has satisfied at least 7 of 8 
best practices criteria; and; 
WHEREAS, eligibility for the incentive payment is contingent 
upon adopting a resolution that states the  District has complied with 
at least 7 of 8 of the best practices criteria;  
130
 8. A.           02.04.14 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED AS FOLLOWS: 
A. The Board of Education of  Troy School District certifies 
that the District has complied with the following 
requirements: 
1) The District is the designated policy holder for 
medical benefit plan(s) pursuant to Section 22f(1)(a). 
2) The District has obtained a competitive bid on non -
instructional services pursuant to Section 22f(1)(b). 
i. This includes custodial services, transportation 
and food services. 
3) The district accepts applications for enrollment by 
non-resident applicants under Section 105 or 105c 
(MCL 388.1705) pursuant to Section 22f(1)(c). 
4) The district supports opportunities for pupils to 
receive postsecondary credit while at tending 
secondary school pursuant to Section 22f(1)(e). 
5) The district offers online courses or blended learning 
opportunities to all eligible pupils , and publishes 
course syllabi pursuant to Section 22f(1)(f). 
6) The District provides a link on the District’ s home 
page to the URL for the MiSchool Data Portal which 
contains the required dashboard indicators pursuant 
to Section 22f(1)(g).  If certain data elements for our 
District are unavailable from State data collections, 
we agree to provide those data in th e form and 
manner determined by the MDE.   
131
 8. A.           02.04.14 
7) The district provides health education and physical 
education pursuant to Section 22f(1)(h). 
B. The Board of Education of Troy School District authorizes 
and directs its secretary to file this resolution with the 
State Aid and School Finance Office of the Michigan 
Department of Education. 
C. All resolutions and parts of resolutions insofar as they 
conflict with the provisions of this resolution are hereby 
rescinded.

 B. CONSIDERATION:  PURCHASE OF BUSES 
  Eighteen of the district’s school buses have reached end -
of-life.  Current school bus pricing on the MSBO/Aggregate 
School Bus Purchasing Program for buses was extended by 
Thomas through February 15, 2014.  Pricing from Hoekstra 
(Thomas) was compared and reviewed with pricing from 
Capital City (IC) and Holland  (Bluebird) by  Mr. Dominick 
Asaro, Transportation Supervisor.  It is recommended the 
bid be awarded as stated below.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Acceptance of the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
Moved By: ______________________________ 
Supported By: ______________________________ 
Be it therefore resolved that the bid for school buses be awarded 
to Hoekstra Transportation (Thomas) in the amount of $1,659,011.00 
and that this amount be approved as an expenditure of the 201 4 
Capital Projects Fund.

141
Memorandum 
 DATE: February 4, 2014 
Services 
 FROM: Mark S. Rajter, Business Services 
4400 Livernois 
Troy, MI 48098 
248/823-4022  
 RE: Bus purchases from the 2014 Bond proceeds   
Fax - 248/823-4013 
It is my recommendation that the board authorizes administration to move forward with the 
purchase of buses to replace existing ones in the District’s fleet which have reached end-of-
life. The recommendation includes purchasing all 19 replacement buses this year from Series 
1 Bond proceeds to obtain the lowest cost, which will be charged to our Bond program. 
Originally, our Bond bus replacement forecast called for 19 buses to be replaced in Series 1 
over a three (3) year period (FY14, 15, 16). The current year MSBO bid price will expire at 
the end of January 2014. Manufacturers have indicated that the price of a bus will increase 
with the 2014-15 MSBO bid award due to material/labor increases.  
It is recommended that the District take advantage of current bus prices by replacing 18 units 
from Series 1 now. Placing an order early in the calendar year will ensure that the units will 
be delivered and ready for the start of the 2014-15 school year. One (1) additional bus will be 
recommended at a later date this spring. 
We have obtained a letter from bus manufacturer, Thomas, to honor the current pricing 
through February 15, 2014. The attached schedules identify the 18 units to be replaced.  
Below is a summary:
